→ Continue\"\n#       scarica kaggle.json (finisce in ~/Downloads del nodo col browser) → ~/.kaggle/kaggle.json (chmod 600)\n#       evitare \"Generate New Token\": mostra il token in UI; col file scaricato il segreto non passa mai dalla chat\n.venv/bin/python -m kaggle config view   # deve mostrare username: sgrivett\n```\n- Login browser: \"Sign in with Google\" → password dal vault (il vault riempie, NON invia: cliccare tu \"Next\") →\n  2FA preferendo «Tap Yes on your phone»: cliccare il `div[role=\"link\"]` dell'opzione (non il contenitore `li`);\n  la pagina (`challenge/dp`, «Check your phone») avanza da sola all'approvazione dal telefono.\n  Password/codici: SEMPRE via vault, mai digitati a mano (dettagli: skill `vault-browser-login`).\n- Segreti: la API key dell'endpoint sta in `~/.kaggle-tpu-lab.json`; mai stamparla in chat.\n- Se auth CLI fallisce: rigenerare \"Create Legacy API Key\" e rimpiazzare kaggle.json.\n\n## Operazioni\n```bash\ncd ~/kaggle-tpu-lab\n.venv/bin/python launch.py serve --keepalive-min 240 > ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log 2>&1   # pusha kernel sgrivett/qwen38-tpu-serve (TPU v5e-8)\n# NB: il redirect su serve.log è OBBLIGATORIO — il waiter grepa quel file (vedi Pitfall).\n.venv/bin/python launch.py status                      # stato + eventi (ri-attach dopo Ctrl-C)\n.venv/bin/python launch.py stop                        # spegne la sessione TPU (non bruciare quota)\n```\n- Coda per uno slot TPU: molto variabile — da minuti a **molte ore** (si allunga di sera/notte CEST = picco USA; nei crunch ufficiali si superano le 20h — vedi Pitfall). **In coda non si consuma quota**; non rilanciare una richiesta in attesa (riparte da zero in coda).\n- Avvio ~22 min (pesi 55GB + compilazione + tunnel); anche l'avvio conta sulla quota.\n- Endpoint: banner \"YOUR ENDPOINT IS LIVE\" in `serve.log` (base URL + api key), oppure\n  `grep -E \"base URL|model\" ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log`. Stato: `~/.kaggle-tpu-lab.json` (kernel, topic ntfy, api_key).\n- Ctrl-C del watcher NON ferma il server (resta su Kaggle); si ferma con `stop` o a fine keepalive (auto-shutdown).\n- Per la sveglia «quando è live»: il watcher di `launch.py` esce solo a fine sessione → usare `scripts/wait_until_live.sh` (questa skill) lanciato in background con notify: grepa `serve.log` per `YOUR ENDPOINT IS LIVE` o `Endpoint URL reserved` (URL assegnato; esce 1 anche su `tunnel-failed`/`PUBLIC TUNNEL NOT UP`) e non stampa mai la api key. Il waiter scade col suo timeout (default 5h30, `exit 2`): se scade e la coda continua, rilanciarlo — nulla si perde. Se muore anche il processo `serve` (di cui `serve.log` è la pipe), ri-attaccare con `launch.py status`.\n\n### Avvio a mano (browser) — se l'utente lo chiede\nLa coda è UNA e non si scavalca: una richiesta manuale mentre un'altra è già in coda riparte **da zero** (si perde l'anzianità di quella in attesa). Percorso manuale legittimo (quando non c'è già una richiesta in coda o l'utente lo chiede esplicitamente):\n1. kaggle.com → Code → il proprio kernel: l'editor è in «draft» — aprirlo NON avvia sessioni e non consuma quota.\n2. Session options: **TPU VM v5e-8** + Internet ON (i dataset restano allegati).\n3. `Save & Run All` = lo stesso commit che pusha `launch.py serve`; `run a cell` = sessione interattiva: solo lì si vede la posizione in coda («sei #N»), ma ha un timer di inattività (~20–30 min) → NON adatta a tenere su un server.\n4. Mai due richieste TPU in parallelo dallo stesso account: se entrambe partono, la quota brucia doppia.\n\n## Uso dall'agent (curl / client OpenAI-compat)\n```bash\nBASE=$(grep -oE 'https://[a-z0-9-]+\\.trycloudflare\\.com/v1' ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log | tail -1)\nKEY=$(python3 -c \"import json;print(json.load(open('$HOME/.kaggle-tpu-lab.json'))['api_key'])\")\ncurl \"$BASE/chat/completions\" -H \"Authorization: Bearer $KEY\" -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -d '{\"model\":\"qwen3.8-27b\",\"messages\":[{\"role\":\"user\",\"content\":\"ping\"}]}'\n```\n- opencode / Hermes: provider custom OpenAI-compatible (baseURL=$BASE, apiKey=[REDATTO] URL nuovo a ogni avvio → riconfigurare il client ad ogni lancio.\n- Prova rapida pronta all'uso: `bash scripts/try_tpu.sh \"prompt\"` (questa skill) — risposta + durata, key mai stampata; se il log non ha ancora un endpoint esce 2 con messaggio chiaro.\n\n## Pitfall\n- **serve.log = redirect stdout di `launch.py serve`**: lanciarlo SEMPRE con `> ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log 2>&1`; senza redirect il file non esiste e il waiter non vede mai il banner. A sessione già avviata NON si rinvia (la coda riparte da zero): se il redirect è sbagliato, collegare serve.log al log vivo con hardlink (`ln <log-vivo> ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log`) — il processo continua a scrivere sullo stesso inode.\n- **`tunnel-failed` / «PUBLIC TUNNEL NOT UP» (sessione viva ma irraggiungibile)**: nel codice di serie cloudflared parte una volta sola (attesa 180 s); se fallisce, la sessione serve solo dentro il kernel e brucia quota. Il clone locale è patchato: **4 tentativi (quic/http2 × edge-ip auto/4) + supervisore in background** che ripubblica `tunnel-url` appena riesce (riga `Endpoint URL reserved`, anche in ritardo). Se il tunnel non sale mai, valuta `stop` per non bruciare quota; non rilanciare `serve` (la coda riparte da zero).\n- Kaggle AUP: keep-alive 24/7 = zona grigia (\"server farming\"); spegnere quando si finisce.\n- **Coda TPU: può durare MOLTE ore** (Kaggle ha confermato ufficialmente i periodi di crunch: poche dozzine di TPU condivise + hackathon ricorrenti; osservate attese oltre 20h). \"tags\": [\"kaggle\", \"tpu\", \"llm-serving\", \"free-tier\", \"openai-compatible\"], \"related_skills\": [], \"content\": \"---\\nname: kaggle-free-ai-server\\ndescription: Use when launching the free Kaggle TPU AI server.\\nversion: 1.0.0\\nauthor: Hermes (Denaro ops)\\nlicense: MIT\\nmetadata:\\n  hermes:\\n    tags: [kaggle, tpu, llm-serving, free-tier, openai-compatible]\\n    related_skills: []\\n---\\n\\n# Kaggle free AI server (kaggle-tpu-lab)\\n\\nQwen3.8-27B (bf16, ctx 262k) servito su **TPU v5e-8 gratuita** di Kaggle, con endpoint\\nOpenAI-compatibile + API key via tunnel Cloudflare. Montato su mc2.\\nUsalo come LABORATORIO: accendi quando serve, spegni quando no (quota settimanale).\\n\\n## When to Use\\n- Serve accendere/spegnere/controllare l'endpoint AI gratuito di Kaggle (Qwen su TPU) o collegarlo a opencode/Hermes.\\n- Serve capire quote e limiti (TPU 20h/sett, GPU 30h/sett) o rifare l'installazione su un altro nodo.\\n\\n## Account e quote (verificati)\\n- Kaggle account: **sgrivett** (login Google `sergio.grivetto@gmail.com`; telefono + identità già verificati).\\n- Quote free settimanali: **TPU 20h** (v5e-8 = 8 chip, 128GB) + **GPU 30h** (2× T4). Controllo: kaggle.com/settings → Quotas.\\n- La variante buona è la **TPU v5e-8** (~130 tok/s, prefill ~10k tok/s). I 2×T4 sono la trappola lenta\\n  (~16 tok/s, modello splittato): non riprodurli salvo richiesta esplicita.\\n\\n## Installazione (fatta su mc2; rifarla solo se manca)\\n```bash\\ngit clone https://github.com/ARahim3/kaggle-tpu-lab ~/kaggle-tpu-lab   # MIT, solo stdlib\\ncd ~/kaggle-tpu-lab && python3 -m venv .venv && .venv/bin/pip install kaggle\\n# auth: kaggle.com/settings → API → \\\"Create Legacy API Key\\\" → conferma \\\"Are you sure? → Continue\\\"\\n#       scarica kaggle.json (finisce in ~/Downloads del nodo col browser) → ~/.kaggle/kaggle.json (chmod 600)\\n#       evitare \\\"Generate New Token\\\": mostra il token in UI; col file scaricato il segreto non passa mai dalla chat\\n.venv/bin/python -m kaggle config view   # deve mostrare username: sgrivett\\n```\\n- Login browser: \\\"Sign in with Google\\\" → password dal vault (il vault riempie, NON invia: cliccare tu \\\"Next\\\") →\\n  2FA preferendo «Tap Yes on your phone»: cliccare il `div[role=\\\"link\\\"]` dell'opzione (non il contenitore `li`);\\n  la pagina (`challenge/dp`, «Check your phone») avanza da sola all'approvazione dal telefono.\\n  Password/codici: SEMPRE via vault, mai digitati a mano (dettagli: skill `vault-browser-login`).\\n- Segreti: la API key dell'endpoint sta in `~/.kaggle-tpu-lab.json`; mai stamparla in chat.\\n- Se auth CLI fallisce: rigenerare \\\"Create Legacy API Key\\\" e rimpiazzare kaggle.json.\\n\\n## Operazioni\\n```bash\\ncd ~/kaggle-tpu-lab\\n.venv/bin/python launch.py serve --keepalive-min 240 > ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log 2>&1   # pusha kernel sgrivett/qwen38-tpu-serve (TPU v5e-8)\\n# NB: il redirect su serve.log è OBBLIGATORIO — il waiter grepa quel file (vedi Pitfall).\\n.venv/bin/python launch.py status                      # stato + eventi (ri-attach dopo Ctrl-C)\\n.venv/bin/python launch.py stop                        # spegne la sessione TPU (non bruciare quota)\\n```\\n- Coda per uno slot TPU: molto variabile — da minuti a **molte ore** (si allunga di sera/notte CEST = picco USA; nei crunch ufficiali si superano le 20h — vedi Pitfall). **In coda non si consuma quota**; non rilanciare una richiesta in attesa (riparte da zero in coda).\\n- Avvio ~22 min (pesi 55GB + compilazione + tunnel); anche l'avvio conta sulla quota.\\n- Endpoint: banner \\\"YOUR ENDPOINT IS LIVE\\\" in `serve.log` (base URL + api key), oppure\\n  `grep -E \\\"base URL|model\\\" ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log`. Stato: `~/.kaggle-tpu-lab.json` (kernel, topic ntfy, api_key).\\n- Ctrl-C del watcher NON ferma il server (resta su Kaggle); si ferma con `stop` o a fine keepalive (auto-shutdown).\\n- Per la sveglia «quando è live»: il watcher di `launch.py` esce solo a fine sessione → usare `scripts/wait_until_live.sh` (questa skill) lanciato in background con notify: grepa `serve.log` per `YOUR ENDPOINT IS LIVE` o `Endpoint URL reserved` (URL assegnato; esce 1 anche su `tunnel-failed`/`PUBLIC TUNNEL NOT UP`) e non stampa mai la api key. Il waiter scade col suo timeout (default 5h30, `exit 2`): se scade e la coda continua, rilanciarlo — nulla si perde. Se muore anche il processo `serve` (di cui `serve.log` è la pipe), ri-attaccare con `launch.py status`.\\n\\n### Avvio a mano (browser) — se l'utente lo chiede\\nLa coda è UNA e non si scavalca: una richiesta manuale mentre un'altra è già in coda riparte **da zero** (si perde l'anzianità di quella in attesa). Percorso manuale legittimo (quando non c'è già una richiesta in coda o l'utente lo chiede esplicitamente):\\n1. kaggle.com → Code → il proprio kernel: l'editor è in «draft» — aprirlo NON avvia sessioni e non consuma quota.\\n2. Session options: **TPU VM v5e-8** + Internet ON (i dataset restano allegati).\\n3. `Save & Run All` = lo stesso commit che pusha `launch.py serve`; `run a cell` = sessione interattiva: solo lì si vede la posizione in coda («sei #N»), ma ha un timer di inattività (~20–30 min) → NON adatta a tenere su un server.\\n4. Mai due richieste TPU in parallelo dallo stesso account: se entrambe partono, la quota brucia doppia.\\n\\n## Uso dall'agent (curl / client OpenAI-compat)\\n```bash\\nBASE=$(grep -oE 'https://[a-z0-9-]+\\\\.trycloudflare\\\\.com/v1' ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log | tail -1)\\nKEY=$(python3 -c \\\"import json;print(json.load(open('$HOME/.kaggle-tpu-lab.json'))['api_key'])\\\")\\ncurl \\\"$BASE/chat/completions\\\" -H \\\"Authorization: Bearer $KEY\\\" -H \\\"Content-Type: application/json\\\" \\\\\\n  -d '{\\\"model\\\":\\\"qwen3.8-27b\\\",\\\"messages\\\":[{\\\"role\\\":\\\"user\\\",\\\"content\\\":\\\"ping\\\"}]}'\\n```\\n- opencode / Hermes: provider custom OpenAI-compatible (baseURL=$BASE, apiKey=[REDATTO] URL nuovo a ogni avvio → riconfigurare il client ad ogni lancio.\\n\\n## Pitfall\\n- **serve.log = redirect stdout di `launch.py serve`**: lanciarlo SEMPRE con `> ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log 2>&1`; senza redirect il file non esiste e il waiter non vede mai il banner. A sessione già avviata NON si rinvia (la coda riparte da zero): se il redirect è sbagliato, collegare serve.log al log vivo con hardlink (`ln <log-vivo> ~/kaggle-tpu-lab/serve.log`) — il processo continua a scrivere sullo stesso inode.\\n- **`tunnel-failed` / «PUBLIC TUNNEL NOT UP» (sessione viva ma irraggiungibile)**: nel codice di serie cloudflared parte una volta sola (attesa 180 s); se fallisce, la sessione serve solo dentro il kernel e brucia quota. Il clone locale è patchato: **4 tentativi (quic/http2 × edge-ip auto/4) + supervisore in background** che ripubblica `tunnel-url` appena riesce (riga `Endpoint URL reserved`, anche in ritardo). Se il tunnel non sale mai, valuta `stop` per non bruciare quota; non rilanciare `serve` (la coda riparte da zero).\\n- Kaggle AUP: keep-alive 24/7 = zona grigia (\\\"server farming\\\"); spegnere quando si finisce.\\n- **Coda TPU: può durare MOLTE ore** (Kaggle ha confermato ufficialmente i periodi di crunch: poche dozzine di TPU condivise + hackathon ricorrenti; osservate attese oltre 20h). In coda NON si consuma quota e il kernel parte da solo appena libera; il watcher è solo comodità — il kernel vive lato Kaggle e `launch.py status` ri-aggancia. Senza cambi di stato non c'è nulla da fare: niente ETA promesse, niente richieste doppie (vedi «Avvio a mano»). **Il log è a eventi, non un heartbeat: `launch.py` stampa «queued» solo ai cambi di stato, quindi una coda di ore lascia `serve.log` muto — silenzio ≠ blocco: verifica con `launch.py status`, non rilanciare `serve` per «riparare» (la coda ripartirebbe da zero).**\\n- L'account serve **KYC completo via Persona**: la sola verifica del telefono non abilita la TPU.\\n- Non è \\\"locale\\\": gira su infra Google; tunnel Cloudflare quick (test/dev, senza garanzie).\\n- Keepalive Qwen runs on\\nvllm-tpu with one patch. GLM-5.3-Flash runs on an engine we wrote in JAX for it; as far as we\\nknow it is the first to run that model on a TPU.\\n\\n## What you need\\n\\nA Kaggle account with TPU access (verify it under Settings: a phone number, and for some accounts an identity check through Persona before TPU sessions are handed out) and its free quota,\\naround 20 TPU hours a week. Nothing to install for the notebook route. For the terminal\\nroute, Python 3.9+ and the Kaggle CLI.\\n\\n## How a session works\\n\\nThe notebook attaches public datasets holding the weights (and, where it helps, a\\npre-built compile cache), builds the engine across the eight chips, opens a Cloudflare\\ntunnel and prints the URL and an API key. The last cell is the server: leave it running.\\nA keepalive holds the session up to Kaggle's limit, a little under nine hours, after\\nwhich you run it again and get a new URL.\\n\\nWire a coding agent with one line, for example Claude Code:\\n\\n```bash\\nANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=<url> ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=<key> ANTHROPIC_MODEL=<model> claude\\n```\\n\\nEach folder README has the exact lines for Claude Code, Codex CLI and opencode.\\n\\n## From a terminal\\n\\n```bash\\ngit clone https://github.com/ARahim3/kaggle-tpu-lab\\ncd kaggle-tpu-lab\\npython launch.py serve            # Qwen3.8-27B today; --model picks another recipe once there is one\\n```\\n\\n`launch.py` pushes the kernel with the Kaggle CLI and follows its progress; `status`\\nand `stop` do what they say.\\n\\n## On a Google Cloud TPU VM\\n\\nThe GLM-5.3-Flash recipe also runs on a rented TPU VM, the same machine as Kaggle's (`v5litepod-8`) or a `v6e-8`,\\nwith no queue and no nine-hour cap: [gcp/](gcp/) has the guide and the three scripts (environment, datasets, run).\\n\\n## Adding a model\\n\\nOne folder at the top level, named after the model: `README.md` with the numbers and the\\nhow, `kernel/` with the serving script, `notebook/` with the run-all notebook generated\\nfrom it, plus whatever the recipe needs (a patch, an engine). The launcher and the\\nnotebook share the same config block, so a setting changed in one place means the same\\nthing in the other.\\n\\n## License\\n\\nThe code here is MIT. Stato runtime in `prove/caccia_continua/`.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\\nfrom typing import Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple\\n\\nfrom ..statistica import sharpe_deflazionato\\nfrom . import scansione as S\\nfrom .scansione3 import vicini_config\\n\\nVERSIONE_STATO = 1\\n\\n#: Configurazioni per giro (default del runner; il lotto vero sta nello stato).\\nBATCH_DEFAULT = 96\\n\\n#: Priorita' di ripiego per un genitore senza expectancy di addestramento valutabile.\\nPRIO_NULL = -1.0\\n\\n#: Limiti DICHIARATI per famiglia/parametro (inclusivi). Un vicino fuori limite non entra.\\n#: `None` = parametro senza limiti numerici (i valori ammessi sono controllati a parte).\\nLIMITI: Dict[str, Dict[str, Optional[Tuple[float, float]]]] = {\\n    \\\"sma_cross\\\": {\\\"fast\\\": (2, 300), \\\"slow\\\": (3, 500)},\\n    \\\"ema_cross\\\": {\\\"fast\\\": (2, 300), \\\"slow\\\": (3, 500)},\\n    \\\"macd\\\": {\\\"fast\\\": (2, 80), \\\"slow\\\": (3, 160), \\\"segnale\\\": (2, 80)},\\n    \\\"mom_abs\\\": {\\\"L\\\": (2, 600)},\\n    \\\"mom_trend\\\": {\\\"L\\\": (2, 600), \\\"sma\\\": (10, 500)},\\n    \\\"donchian\\\": {\\\"n_in\\\": (2, 400), \\\"n_out\\\": (1, 300)},\\n    \\\"reversion_z\\\": {\\\"n\\\": (2, 300), \\\"k\\\": (0.25, 5.0)},\\n    \\\"rsi2\\\": {\\\"periodo\\\": (2, 12), \\\"soglia_in\\\": (1, 45), \\\"soglia_out\\\": (50, 99)},\\n    \\\"turn_month\\\": {\\\"k\\\": (1, 6), \\\"pos\\\": None},\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef chiave(cfg: dict) -> str:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Etichetta stabile di una configurazione (stessa convenzione delle scansioni).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return S.chiave_config(cfg)\\n\\n\\ndef dentro_limiti(cfg: dict) -> bool:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"La configurazione rispetta i limiti dichiarati? (chiavi ignote = fuori).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    limiti = LIMITI.get(str(cfg.get(\\\"famiglia\\\", \\\"\\\")))\\n    if limiti is None:\\n        return False\\n    for nome, valore in cfg.items():\\n (\\\"primi\\\", \\\"ultimi\\\"):\\n                    return False\\n                continue\\n            return False\\n        minimo, massimo = bound\\n        try:\\n            if not (minimo <= float(valore) <= massimo):\\n                return False\\n        except (TypeError, ValueError):\\n            return False\\n    return True\\n\\n\\ndef vicini_caccia(cfg: dict) -> List[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Vicini ±1 passo della configurazione, entro i limiti dichiarati, dedup per chiave.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    visti: set = set()\\n    fuori: List[dict] = []\\n    for vicino in vicini_config(cfg):\\n        if not dentro_limiti(vicino):\\n            continue\\n        k = chiave(vicino)\\n        if k in visti:\\n            continue\\n        visti.add(k)\\n        fuori.append(vicino)\\n    return fuori\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass Accumulo:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Somme sufficienti dei tentativi cumulativi: n, somma e somma dei quadrati.\\n\\n    La varianza e' quella di popolazione, perche' la definizione del progetto\\n    (`varianza_da_tentativi`) e' quella di popolazione — qui si mantiene in forma\\n    incrementale, non si ricostruisce la lista.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    n: int = 0\\n    somma: float = 0.0\\n    somma2: float = 0.0\\n\\n    def aggiungi(self, sharpe: Optional[float]) -> None:\\n        if sharpe is None:\\n            return\\n        self.n += 1\\n        self.somma += float(sharpe)\\n        self.somma2 += float(sharpe) * float(sharpe)\\n\\n    @property\\n    def varianza(self) -> float:\\n        if self.n < 2:\\n            return 0.0\\n        media = self.somma / self.n\\n        return max(self.somma2 / self.n - media * media, 0.0)\\n\\n    def a_dict(self) -> dict:\\n        return {\\\"n\\\": self.n, \\\"somma\\\": self.somma, \\\"somma2\\\": self.somma2}\\n\\n    @classmethod\\n    def da_dict(cls, d: Optional[dict]) -> \\\"Accumulo\\\":\\n        d = d or {}\\n        return cls(n=int(d.get(\\\"n\\\") or 0), somma=float(d.get(\\\"somma\\\") or 0.0),\\n                   somma2=float(d.get(\\\"somma2\\\") or 0.0))\\n\\n\\ndef aggiorna_accumulo(acc: Accumulo, records: Sequence[dict]) -> None:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Fonde nei conti cumulativi le somme per-configurazione dei record di un giro.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    for rec in records:\\n        stat = rec.get(\\\"stat\\\") or {}\\n        acc.n += int(stat.get(\\\"n\\\") or 0)\\n        acc.somma += float(stat.get(\\\"somma\\\") or 0.0)\\n        acc.somma2 += float(stat.get(\\\"somma2\\\") or 0.0)\\n\\n\\ndef semi_da_artefatti(documenti: Sequence[dict]) -> Tuple[Dict[str, dict], Dict[str, Optional[float]]]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Dai documenti-artefatto delle scansioni a (semi, priorita').\\n\\n    `semi`: chiave -> configurazione (famiglia + parametri). `priorita'`: chiave -> migliore\\n    expectancy di ADDESTRAMENTO tra i tentativi valutabili (None se nessuno). La verifica non\\n    viene letta: serve solo per l'ordinamento della frontiera.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    semi: Dict[str, dict] = {}\\n    priori: Dict[str, Optional[float]] = {}\\n    for doc in documenti:\\n        trials = ((doc or {}).get(\\\"results\\\") or {}).get(\\\"trials\\\") or []\\n        for t in trials:\\n            k = str(t[\\\"chiave\\\"])\\n            if k not in semi:\\n                semi[k] = {\\\"famiglia\\\": t[\\\"famiglia\\\"], **(t.get(\\\"params\\\") or {})}\\n            train = t.get(\\\"train\\\") or {}\\n            if (train.get(\\\"n\\\") or 0) >= S.MIN_OP and train.get(\\\"expectancy\\\") is not None:\\n                e = float(train[\\\"expectancy\\\"])\\n                if k not in priori or priori[k] is None or e > priori[k]:\\n                    priori[k] = e\\n    return semi, priori\\n\\n\\ndef nuova_frontiera(coda: List[list], viste: set, cfg: dict, prio: Optional[float]) -> int:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Aggiunge alla coda i vicini ±1 passo non ancora visti; ritorna quanti ne ha aggiunti.\\n\\n    Ogni voce della coda e' `[chiave, config, priorita']` (JSON-friendly).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    p = PRIO_NULL if prio is None else float(prio)\\n    aggiunti = 0\\n    for vicino in vicini_caccia(cfg):\\n        k = chiave(vicino)\\n        if k in viste:\\n            continue\\n        viste.add(k)\\n        coda.append([k, vicino, p])\\n        aggiunti += 1\\n    return aggiunti\\n\\n\\ndef estrai_batch(coda: List[list], n: int) -> List[list]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Estrae (e rimuove) fino a `n` voci a priorita' piu' alta: best-first, pareggio per chiave.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if n <= 0:\\n        return []\\n    coda.sort(key=lambda voce: (-float(voce[2]), str(voce[0])))\\n    fuori = coda[:n]\\n    del coda[:n]\\n    return fuori\\n\\n\\ndef record_da_trials(trials: Sequence[dict], *, min_op: int = S.MIN_OP) -> List[dict]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Dai trial di un giro ai record per-configurazione (uno per chiave, ordine stabile).\\n\\n    Ogni record: chiave, famiglia, parametri, le somme sufficienti dei tentativi valutabili\\n    (`stat`) e la selezione di addestramento (`sel`: miglior simbolo per expectancy tra i\\n    tentativi con n_train >= min_op; None se nessuno). La verifica entra solo dentro `sel`,\\n    per il ricontrollo dei candidati — mai per scegliere.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    per_config: Dict[str, List[dict]] = {}\\n    for t in trials:\\n        per_config.setdefault(str(t[\\\"chiave\\\"]), []).append(t)\\n    fuori: List[dict] = []\\n    for k in sorted(per_config):\\n        gruppo = per_config[k]\\n        acc = Accumulo()\\n        for t in gruppo:\\n            train = t.get(\\\"train\\\") or {}\\n            if (train.get(\\\"n\\\") or 0) >= min_op:\\n                acc.aggiungi(train.get(\\\"sharpe\\\"))\\n        ammessi = [t for t in gruppo\\n                   if (t[\\\"train\\\"].get(\\\"n\\\") or 0) >= min_op\\n                   and t[\\\"train\\\"].get(\\\"expectancy\\\") is not None]\\n        sel = None\\n        if ammessi:\\n            scelta = max(ammessi, key=lambda t: t[\\\"train\\\"][\\\"expectancy\\\"])\\n            campi = (\\\"n\\\", \\\"expectancy\\\", \\\"sharpe\\\", \\\"t_stat\\\")\\n            sel = {\\n                \\\"simbolo\\\": scelta[\\\"simbolo\\\"],\\n                \\\"train\\\": {c: scelta[\\\"train\\\"].get(c) for c in campi},\\n                \\\"oos\\\": {c: scelta[\\\"oos\\\"].get(c) for c in campi},\\n            }\\n        fuori.append({\\n            \\\"chiave\\\": k,\\n            \\\"famiglia\\\": gruppo[0][\\\"famiglia\\\"],\\n            \\\"params\\\": gruppo[0].get(\\\"params\\\") or {},\\n            \\\"stat\\\": acc.a_dict(),\\n            \\\"sel\\\": sel,\\n        })\\n    return fuori\\n\\n\\ndef papabile(sel: Optional[dict], *, min_op: int = S.MIN_OP) -> bool:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Screening in verifica (identico alle scansioni): n >= soglia, exp > 0, t > 0.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if not sel:\\n        return False\\n    oos = sel.get(\\\"oos\\\") or {}\\n    return ((oos.get(\\\"n\\\") or 0) >= min_op\\n            and (oos.get(\\\"expectancy\\\") or 0.0) > 0.0\\n            and (oos.get(\\\"t_stat\\\") or 0.0) > 0.0)\\n\\n\\ndef dsr_di(sel: dict, accumulo: Accumulo) -> Optional[float]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"DSR della selezione col conteggio cumulativo corrente; None se non calcolabile.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    oos = sel.get(\\\"oos\\\") or {}\\n    n_oos = int(oos.get(\\\"n\\\") or 0)\\n    if n_oos < 2 or oos.get(\\\"sharpe\\\") is None or accumulo.n < 2:\\n        return None\\n    return sharpe_deflazionato(float(oos[\\\"sharpe\\\"]), n_oos, accumulo.n,\\n                               accumulo.varianza)[\\\"dsr\\\"]\\n\\n\\ndef valuta_candidati(papabili: Dict[str, dict], candidati: Dict[str, dict],\\n                     accumulo: Accumulo, *, soglia: float = S.SOGLIA_DSR) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Transizioni di stato rispetto al conteggio cumulativo corrente.\\n\\n    Ritorna `{\\\"promossi\\\": [(chiave, dsr)...], \\\"ripromossi\\\": [...], \\\"declassati\\\": [...]}`:\\n    - promosso: passava il DSR >= soglia e non era mai stato candidato;\\n    - ripromosso: era declassato e torna a passare;\\n    - declassato: era candidato \\\"ok\\\" e col conteggio salito non passa piu'.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    promossi: List[tuple] = []\\n    ripromossi: List[tuple] = []\\n    declassati: List[tuple] = []\\n    for k in sorted(papabili):\\n        dsr = dsr_di(papabili[k], accumulo)\\n        passato = dsr is not None and dsr >= soglia\\n        stato = ripromossi.append((k, dsr))\\n        elif not passato and stato is not None and str(stato.get(\\\"stato\\\")) == \\\"ok\\\":\\n            declassati.append((k, dsr))\\n    return {\\\"promossi\\\": "arguments": "{\"content\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Caccia continua (S4) — un giro: valuta il prossimo lotto di configurazioni MAI testate.\\n\\nDirettiva del proprietario: «metti a Il come e' dichiarato una volta sola in `src/money/ricerca/caccia_continua.py`\\n(spazio, limiti, ordine best-first, contabilita' DSR cumulativa, ricontrollo dei candidati)\\ne non si tocca piu'.\\n\\nUn giro: estrae il lotto migliore dalla frontiera -> valuta su 16 major (costi reali,\\nfinestra aggiornata a IERI) -> aggiorna i conti cumulativi -> espande la frontiera coi\\nvicini dei valutati -> promuove/declassa candidati -> scrive stato, evidenza e log.\\n\\nNON promuove nulla in produzione: i candidati si pre-registrano come esperimenti e\\npassano dal cancello (spec -> misura -> cancello).\\n\\nUso:   .venv/bin/python scripts/caccia_continua.py [--batch 96] [--fine 2026-10-07]\\n       .venv/bin/python scripts/caccia_continua.py --prova        # nessuna scrittura\\n       .venv/bin/python scripts/caccia_continua.py --mostra-stato\\nCron:  */10 su mc2 (flock). \\\"money\\\" / \\\"__init__.py\\\").exists():\\n        if str(_src) not in sys.path:\\n            sys.path.insert(0, str(_src))\\n        if str(_antenato) not in sys.path:\\n            sys.path.insert(0, str(_antenato))\\n        break\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))\\n\\nfrom money.costi import get_tariffa  # noqa: E402\\nfrom money.dati import Scarica E402\\nfrom scansione import SIMBOLI, SLIPPAGE_PER_LATO, TARIFFA, carica_universo  # noqa: E402\\n\\nRADICE = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\\nDIR = RADICE / \\\"prove\\\" / \\\"scansioni\\\"\\nREGISTRO = RADICE / \\\"prove\\\" / \\\"REGISTRO_ESPERIMENTI.md\\\"\\nNOTIFICA = RADICE / \\\"ops\\\" / \\\"alerts\\\" / \\\"notifica_tg.py\\\"\\nVENV_PY = RADICE / \\\".venv\\\" / \\\"bin\\\" / \\\"python3\\\"\\n\\n#: Gli artefatti-seme: le configurazioni GIA' viste (S1 + S3 + S3ampio) — l'ultimo di ognuno.\\nSEMI_GLOB = (\\\"scansione_S1_*.json\\\", \\\"scansione_S3_*.json\\\", \\\"scansione_S3ampio_*.json\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef adesso_iso() -> str:\\n    return f\\\"{datetime.now(timezone.utc):%F %T}Z\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef log(msg: str) -> None:\\n    print(f\\\"[{adesso_iso()}] {msg}\\\", flush=True)\\n\\n\\ndef carica_stato() -> dict | None:\\n    if not STATO.exists():\\n        return None\\n    return json.loads(STATO.read_text(encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\"))\\n\\n\\ndef salva_stato(stato: dict) -> None:\\n    DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\\n    tmp = STATO.with_suffix(\\\".json.tmp\\\")\\n    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(stato, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(\\\",\\\", \\\":\\\")),\\n                   encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\")\\n    tmp.replace(STATO)\\n\\n\\ndef notifica(testo: str) -> None:\\n    try:\\n        r = subprocess.run([str(VENV_PY), str(NOTIFICA), testo], capture_output=True,\\n                           text=True, timeout=60)\\n        log(f\\\"notifica: {'ok' if r.returncode == 0 else f'rc={r.returncode}'}\\\")\\n    except Exception as errore:  # noqa: BLE001 — il giro non muore per l'alert\\n        log(f\\\"notifica: eccezione {type(errore).__name__}\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef riga_registro(testo: str) -> None:\\n    with open(REGISTRO, \\\"a\\\", encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\") as f:\\n        f.write(testo)\\n\\n\\ndef json.loads(percorsi[-1].read_text(encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\"))\\n\\n\\ndef inizializza(batch: int) -> dict:\\n    documenti = [d for d in (documento_ultimo(m) for m in SEMI_GLOB) if d is not None]\\n    semi, priori = C.semi_da_artefatti(documenti)\\n    if not semi:\\n        raise RuntimeError(\\\"nessun artefatto-seme trovato in prove/scansioni/\\\")\\n    stato = C.stato_iniziale(semi, priori, creazione=adesso_iso(), batch=batch)\\n    log(f\\\"inizializzata: semi={len(semi)}, frontiera={len(stato['coda'])}\\\")\\n    return stato\\n\\n\\ndef _exp(x) -> str:\\n    return f\\\"{x * 100:+.2f}%\\\" if x is not None else \\\"n/d\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef _n2(x) -> str:\\n    return f\\\"{x:.2f}\\\" if x is not None else \\\"n/d\\\"\\n\\n\\ndef scrivi_candidato(k: str, sel: dict, dsr, acc: = CANDIDATI_DIR / f\\\"{datetime.now(timezone.utc):%Y%m%dT%H%M%S}-{slug}.json\\\"\\n    percorso.write_text(json.dumps(\\n        {\\\"chiave\\\": k, \\\"selezione\\\": sel, \\\"dsr\\\": dsr, C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"sma_cross\\\", \\\"fast\\\": 400, \\\"slow\\\": 500})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"sma_cross\\\", \\\"fast\\\": 10, \\\"slow\\\": 600})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"macd\\\", \\\"fast\\\": 12, \\\"slow\\\": 200, \\\"segnale\\\": 9})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"donchian\\\", \\\"n_in\\\": 450, \\\"n_out\\\": 10})\\n    assert C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"turn_month\\\", \\\"pos\\\": \\\"primi\\\", \\\"k\\\": 6})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"turn_month\\\", \\\"pos\\\": \\\"primi\\\", \\\"k\\\": 7})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"turn_month\\\", \\\"pos\\\": \\\"meta'\\\", \\\"k\\\": 2})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"sconosciuta\\\", \\\"x\\\": 1})\\n    assert not C.dentro_limiti({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"sma_cross\\\", \\\"fast\\\": 10, \\\"slow\\\": 30, \\\"boh\\\": 1})\\n\\n\\ndef test_vicini_dedup_e_dominio():\\n    vicini = C.vicini_caccia({\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"sma_cross\\\", \\\"fast\\\": 10, \\\"slow\\\": 30})\\n    coppie = sorted((v[\\\"fast\\\"], v[\\\"slow\\\"]) for v in vicini)\\n    assert coppie == [(5, 30), (10, 20), (10, 40), (15, 30)]\\n    chiavi = [C.chiave(v) for v in vicini]\\n    assert len(chiavi) == [\\\"a\\\", {\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"mom_abs\\\", \\\"L\\\": 10}, 0.05],\\n            [\\\"c\\\", {\\\"famiglia\\\": \\\"mom_abs\\\", \\\"L\\\": 10}, -0.1],\\n            [\\\"d\\\", {\\\"famiglia\\\": OpenCode is a provider-agnostic, open-source AI coding agent with a TUI and CLI.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- User explicitly asks to use OpenCode\n- You want an external coding agent to implement/refactor/review code\n- You need long-running coding sessions with progress checks\n- You want parallel task execution in isolated workdirs/worktrees\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- OpenCode installed: `npm i -g opencode-ai@latest` or `brew install anomalyco/tap/opencode`\n- Auth configured: `opencode auth login` or set provider env vars (OPENROUTER_API_KEY, etc.)\n- Verify: `opencode auth list` should show at least one provider\n- Git repository for code tasks (recommended)\n- `pty=true` for interactive TUI sessions\n\n## Binary Resolution (Important)\n\nShell environments may resolve different OpenCode binaries. If behavior differs between your terminal and Hermes, check:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"which -a opencode\")\nterminal(command=\"opencode --version\")\n```\n\nIf needed, pin an explicit binary path:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"$HOME/.opencode/bin/opencode run '...'\", workdir=\"~/project\", pty=true)\n```\n\n## One-Shot Tasks\n\nUse `opencode run` for bounded, non-interactive tasks:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Add retry logic to API calls and update tests'\", workdir=\"~/project\")\n```\n\nAttach context files with `-f`:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Review this config for security issues' -f config.yaml -f .env.example\", workdir=\"~/project\")\n```\n\nShow model thinking with `--thinking`:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Debug why tests fail in CI' --thinking\", workdir=\"~/project\")\n```\n\nForce a specific model:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Refactor auth module' --model openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-4\", workdir=\"~/project\")\n```\n\n## Interactive Sessions (Background)\n\nFor iterative work requiring multiple exchanges, start the TUI in background:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode\", workdir=\"~/project\", background=true, pty=true)\n# Returns session_id\n\n# Send a prompt\nprocess(action=\"submit\", session_id=\"<id>\", data=\"Implement OAuth refresh flow and add tests\")\n\n# Monitor progress\nprocess(action=\"poll\", session_id=\"<id>\")\nprocess(action=\"log\", session_id=\"<id>\")\n\n# Send follow-up input\nprocess(action=\"submit\", session_id=\"<id>\", data=\"Now add error handling for token expiry\")\n\n# Exit cleanly — Ctrl+C\nprocess(action=\"write\", session_id=\"<id>\", data=\"\\x03\")\n# Or just kill the process\nprocess(action=\"kill\", session_id=\"<id>\")\n```\n\n**Important:** Do NOT use `/exit` — it is not a valid OpenCode command and will open an agent selector dialog instead. Use Ctrl+C (`\\x03`) or `process(action=\"kill\")` to exit.\n\n### TUI Keybindings\n\n| Key | Action |\n|-----|--------|\n| `Enter` | Submit message (press twice if needed) |\n| `Tab` | Switch between agents (build/plan) |\n| `Ctrl+P` | Open command palette |\n| `Ctrl+X L` | Switch session |\n| `Ctrl+X M` | Switch model |\n| `Ctrl+X N` | New session |\n| `Ctrl+X E` | Open editor |\n| `Ctrl+C` | Exit OpenCode |\n\n### Resuming Sessions\n\nAfter exiting, OpenCode prints a session ID. Resume with:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode -c\", workdir=\"~/project\", background=true, pty=true)  # Continue last session\nterminal(command=\"opencode -s ses_abc123\", workdir=\"~/project\", background=true, pty=true)  # Specific session\n```\n\n## Common Flags\n\n| Flag | Use |\n|------|-----|\n| `run 'prompt'` | One-shot execution and exit |\n| `--continue` / `-c` | Continue the last OpenCode session |\n| `--session <id>` / `-s` | Continue a specific session |\n| `--agent <name>` | Choose OpenCode agent (build or plan) |\n| `--model provider/model` | Force specific model |\n| `--format json` | Machine-readable output/events |\n| `--file <path>` / `-f` | Attach file(s) to the message |\n| `--thinking` | Show model thinking blocks |\n| `--variant <level>` | Reasoning effort (high, max, minimal) |\n| `--title <name>` | Name the session |\n| `--attach <url>` | Connect to a running opencode server |\n\n## Procedure\n\n1. Verify tool readiness:\n   - `terminal(command=\"opencode --version\")`\n   - `terminal(command=\"opencode auth list\")`\n2. For bounded tasks, use `opencode run '...'` (no pty needed).\n3. For iterative tasks, start `opencode` with `background=true, pty=true`.\n4. Monitor long tasks with `process(action=\"poll\"|\"log\")`.\n5. If OpenCode asks for input, respond via `process(action=\"submit\", ...)`.\n6. Exit with `process(action=\"write\", data=\"\\x03\")` or `process(action=\"kill\")`.\n7. Summarize file changes, test results, and next steps back to user.\n\n## PR Review Workflow\n\nOpenCode has a built-in PR command:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode pr 42\", workdir=\"~/project\", pty=true)\n```\n\nOr review in a temporary clone for isolation:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"REVIEW=$(mktemp -d) && git clone https://github.com/user/repo.git $REVIEW && cd $REVIEW && opencode run 'Review this PR vs main. Report bugs, security risks, test gaps, and style issues.' -f $(git diff origin/main --name-only | head -20 | tr '\\n' ' ')\", pty=true)\n```\n\n## Parallel Work Pattern\n\nUse separate workdirs/worktrees to avoid collisions:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Fix issue #101 and commit'\", workdir=\"~/.hermes/cache/scratch/issue-101\", background=true, pty=true)\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Add parser regression tests and commit'\", workdir=\"~/.hermes/cache/scratch/issue-102\", background=true, pty=true)\nprocess(action=\"list\")\n```\n\n## Session & Cost Management\n\nList past sessions:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode session list\")\n```\n\nCheck token usage and costs:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode stats\")\nterminal(command=\"opencode stats --days 7 --models anthropic/claude-sonnet-4\")\n```\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- Interactive `opencode` (TUI) sessions require `pty=true`. The `opencode run` command does NOT need pty.\n- `/exit` is NOT a valid command — it opens an agent selector. Use Ctrl+C to exit the TUI.\n- PATH mismatch can select the wrong OpenCode binary/model config.\n- If OpenCode appears stuck, inspect logs before killing:\n  - `process(action=\"log\", session_id=\"<id>\")`\n- Avoid sharing one working directory across parallel OpenCode sessions.\n- Enter may need to be pressed twice to submit in the TUI (once to finalize text, once to send).\n\n## Verification\n\nSmoke test:\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"opencode run 'Respond with exactly: OPENCODE_SMOKE_OK'\")\n```\n\nSuccess criteria:\n- Output includes `OPENCODE_SMOKE_OK`\n- Command exits without provider/model errors\n- For code tasks: expected files changed and tests pass\n\n## Rules\n\n1. Prefer `opencode run` for one-shot automation — it's simpler and doesn't need pty.\n2. Use interactive background mode only when iteration is needed.\n3.
```json
{
  "provider": {
    "myprovider": {
      "npm": "@ai-sdk/openai-compatible",
      "name": "My Provider",
      "options": {
        "baseURL": "https://...",
        "apiKey": "..."

      }
    }
  }
}
```

This is the documented custom provider format for opencode (docs.opencode.ai / opencode.ai/docs/providers#custom). For OpenAI-compatible endpoints, `npm: "@ai-sdk/openai-compatible"` is right.

The API key is not passed to the provider, resulting in an `Unauthorized` / `Not authenticated` error.\n\nConfigure `opencode.json` with `{env:...}`\n```\n{\n\"$schema\": \"https://opencode.ai/config.json\",\n\"provider\": {\n\"my-provider\": {\n\"npm\": \"@ai-sdk/openai-compatible\",\n\"name\": \"My Provider\",\n\"options\": {\n\"baseURL\": \"https://api.my-provider.example.com/v1\",\n\"apiKey\": \"{env:MY_PROVIDER_API_KEY}\"\n},\n\"models\": {\n\"my-model-large\": {\n\"name\": \"my-model-large\",\n\"limit\": { \"context\": 131072, \"output\": 65536 }\n}\n}\n}\n}\n}\n```\n\n| Method | Result |\n|---|---|\n| `\"apiKey\": \"[TOKEN REDATTO]\"` (hardcoded) | ✅ Works |\n| `\"apiKey\": \"{file:~/.secrets/my-provider-key}\"` | ✅ Works |\n| `\"apiKey\": \"{env:MY_PROVIDER_API_KEY}\"` | ❌ Fails — Unauthorized |\n\n`{env:MY_PROVIDER_API_KEY}` should be substituted with the environment variable value before the API key is passed to the provider SDK, just like `{file:...}` is.\n## Actual Behavior\nThe `{env:...}` token appears to not be resolved (or resolved to an empty string) for custom provider `options.apiKey`. The request is sent without authentication.\n\n## Workaround\nUse `{file:...}` substitution instead:\n```\necho -n \"$MY_PROVIDER_API_KEY\" > ~/.secrets/my-provider-key\nchmod 600 ~/.secrets/my-provider-key\n```\n```\n\"apiKey\": \"{file:~/.secrets/my-provider-key}\"\n```",

Everything else lives in reference files — **load the matching reference (below) before answering**; do not answer detail questions from the body alone.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## Scope & Verification\n\nThis skill is a concise operating guide, not the complete source of truth for every Hermes feature. If a Hermes feature, command, or setting is not mentioned here or in a reference, do not treat that absence as evidence that it does not exist. Check the live repository and official docs before giving a negative answer.\n\nGood verification targets, cheapest first:\n\n- **Every shipped feature, one line each: https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/llms.txt.** Start here for any \"can Hermes do X?\" or \"how do I do X?\" — it indexes the entire documentation set with a link to the page that answers. It is generated from the docs tree on every build, so it is never behind the product. Fetch it with `web_extract`, or `curl -s https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/llms.txt` when web tools are off. The whole documentation set in one file is at `/docs/llms-full.txt`.\n- CLI commands: `hermes --help`, `hermes <command> --help`, and `hermes_cli/main.py`\n- Source tree: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent\n\nNever answer \"Hermes can't do that\" from memory. Hermes ships far more than this skill body describes, and the index exists so a negative answer is always checkable.\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash\n# Install (shell installer — bootstraps PM, Python, dependencies, and the launcher)\ncurl -fsSL https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default surface; set display.interface: tui to launch the Ink TUI instead)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard  /  pick model+provider  /  health check\nhermes setup\nhermes model\nhermes doctor\n\n# Other surfaces\nhermes desktop                 # launch the native desktop app (alias: hermes gui)\nhermes dashboard               # web admin panel + embedded chat\nhermes proxy                   # OpenAI-compatible local proxy backed by your OAuth provider\n```\n\n## Key Paths\n\n```\n~/.hermes/config.yaml       Main configuration (settings — never secrets)\n~/.hermes/.env              API keys and secrets ONLY (under $HERMES_HOME if set)\n$HERMES_HOME/skills/        Installed skills\n~/.hermes/skins/            Custom themes (see references/themes.md)\n~/.hermes/desktop-plugins/  Desktop app UI plugins (see references/desktop-plugins.md)\n~/.hermes/tui-widgets/      TUI widget apps (see references/tui-widgets.md)\n~/.hermes/pets/             Installed pet mascots (see references/petdex.md)\n~/.hermes/state.db          Canonical session store (SQLite + FTS5)\n~/.hermes/sessions/         Gateway routing index, request dumps, *.jsonl transcripts\n~/.hermes/logs/             Gateway and error logs\n~/.hermes/auth.json         OAuth tokens and credential pools\n~/.hermes/hermes-agent/     Source code (if git-installed)\n```\n\nProfiles use `~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/` with the same layout. When a profile is active, resolve the real home from `$HERMES_HOME` — never hardcode `~/.hermes`.\n\n## Routing Table — load the reference for the task\n\n| User wants... | Load |\n|---|---|\n| **Anything not listed below — \"can Hermes do X?\", \"how do I set up X?\"** | **https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/llms.txt** |\n| Bots that chat, run routines, or message each other; the Bots tab | docs: `/user-guide/bot-mode` |\n| CLI commands, subcommands, flags, \"how do I run X\" | `references/cli-reference.md` |\n| In-session slash commands | `references/slash-commands.md` |\n| Provider setup, API keys, OAuth | `references/providers-and-models.md` |\n| config.yaml sections, toolsets, voice/STT/TTS | `references/configuration.md` |\n| AGENTS.md / .hermes.md / CLAUDE.md project rules | `references/project-context-files.md` |\n| Secret redaction, PII, approval modes, \"reset permissions\" | `references/security-privacy.md` |\n| Delegation, cron, curator, kanban | `references/background-systems.md` |\n| MCP servers (add, catalog, `hermes mcp`) | `references/native-mcp.md` |\n| Webhook routes and event-driven runs | `references/webhooks.md` |\n| A custom theme/skin (\"synthwave theme\", \"change the gold ●\") | `references/themes.md` + `templates/skin.yaml` |\n| A desktop app UI element (pane, widget, ⌘K command, page) | `references/desktop-plugins.md` + `templates/plugin.js` |\n| A live TUI panel or modal widget (ticker, clock, dashboard) | `references/tui-widgets.md` + `templates/clock.mjs` |\n| Pet mascots — install, select, scale, diagnose | `references/petdex.md` |\n| Windows-specific issues (keybinds, WinError 10106, BOM) | `references/windows-quirks.md` |\n| Debugging: voice, tools missing, gateway, aux models | `references/troubleshooting.md` |\n| Contributing code: adding tools, slash commands, tests | `references/contributor-guide.md` |\n| delegate_task \"capped at N\" reports | `references/delegate-ta[API-KEY REDATTA].md` |\n| \"Can app X use my Nous Portal subscription/OAuth?\" | `references/portal-auth-for-third-party-apps.md` |\n| Connecting a messaging platform (Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, …) | docs: `/user-guide/messaging` |\n\nThe reference list above is not the feature list — it is the set of topics that\nneed more than their docs page. For everything else Hermes ships, fetch\n`llms.txt` and it maps the question to the page that answers it.\n\nTwo theming rules that hold even without loading the reference: **you apply skins yourself** (`hermes config set display.skin <name>` — every surface repaints live within ~a second; don't tell the user to run `/skin`), and **to tweak one color, edit the ACTIVE skin** (`hermes skin set <key> <hex>`) — never fork `default`, which drops the palette and resets the background.\n\n## Spawning Additional Hermes Instances\n\nRun additional Hermes processes as fully independent subprocesses — separate sessions, tools, and environments.\n\n### When to Use This vs delegate_task\n\n| | `delegate_task` | Spawning `hermes` process |\n|-|-----------------|--------------------------|\n| Isolation | Separate conversation, shared process | Fully independent process |\n| Duration | Minutes (bounded by parent loop) | Hours/days |\n| Tool access | Subset of parent's tools | Full tool access |\n| Interactive | No | Yes (PTY mode) |\n| Use case | Quick parallel subtasks | Long autonomous missions |\n\n### One-Shot Mode\n\n```\nterminal(command=\"hermes chat -q 'Research GRPO papers and write summary to ~/research/grpo.md'\", timeout=300)\n\n# Background for long tasks:\nterminal(command=\"hermes chat -q 'Set up CI/CD for ~/myapp'\", background=true)\n```\n\n### Interactive PTY Mode (via tmux)\n\nHermes uses prompt_toolkit, which requires a real terminal. Use tmux for interactive spawning:\n\n```\n# Start\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s agent1 -x 120 -y 40 'hermes'\", timeout=10)\n\n# Wait for startup, then send a message\nterminal(command=\"sleep 8 && tmux send-keys -t agent1 'Build a FastAPI auth service' Enter\", timeout=15)\n\n# Read output\nterminal(command=\"sleep 20 && tmux capture-pane -t agent1 -p\", timeout=5)\n\n# Send follow-up\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t agent1 'Add rate limiting middleware' Enter\", timeout=5)\n\n# Exit\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t agent1 '/exit' Enter && sleep 2 && tmux kill-session -t agent1\", timeout=10)\n```\n\n### Multi-Agent Coordination\n\n```\n# Agent A: backend\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s backend -x 120 -y 40 'hermes -w'\", timeout=10)\nterminal(command=\"sleep 8 && tmux send-keys -t backend 'Build REST API for user management' Enter\", timeout=15)\n\n# Agent B: frontend\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s frontend -x 120 -y 40 'hermes -w'\", timeout=10)\nterminal(command=\"sleep 8 && tmux send-keys -t frontend 'Build React dashboard for user management' Enter\", timeout=15)\n\n# Check progress, relay context between them\nterminal(command=\"tmux capture-pane -t backend -p | tail -30\", timeout=5)\nterminal(command=\"tmux send-keys -t frontend 'Here is the API schema from the backend agent: ...' Enter\", timeout=5)\n```\n\n### Session Resume\n\n```\n# Resume most recent session\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s resumed 'hermes --continue'\", timeout=10)\n\n# Resume specific session\nterminal(command=\"tmux new-session -d -s resumed 'hermes --resume 20260225_143052_a1b2c3'\", timeout=10)\n```\n\n### Tips\n\n- **Prefer `delegate_task` for quick subtasks** — less overhead than spawning a full process\n- **Use `-w` (worktree mode)** when spawning agents that edit code — prevents git conflicts\n- **Set timeouts** for one-shot mode — complex tasks can take 5-10 minutes\n- **Use `hermes chat -q` for fire-and-forget** — no PTY needed\n- **Use tmux for interactive sessions** — raw PTY mode has `\\r` vs `\\n` issues with prompt_toolkit\n- **For scheduled tasks**, use the `cronjob` tool instead of spawning — handles delivery and retry\n- **\"delegate_task is capped at N\" reports** — see `references/delegate-ta[API-KEY REDATTA].md`. Three real cap paths in Hermes; if none fired, the model is self-limiting and rationalising it as \"the runtime caps.\"\n- **\"Can $external_app use my Nous Portal subscription / OAuth?\"** — see `references/portal-auth-for-third-party-apps.md`. Walk the user through three layers (plugin-vs-app, what Portal actually exposes, local-broker-proxy option).\n\n## Surfaces (quick orientation)\n\n- **Desktop app** (`hermes desktop` / `hermes gui`) — native Electron app for macOS/Linux/Windows: streaming chat, session list, Cmd+K palette, drag-and-drop files, native notifications, per-profile remote-gateway login. Extend it with UI plugins — `references/desktop-plugins.md`.\n- **Web dashboard** (`hermes dashboard`) — full admin panel: messaging channels, MCP catalog, webhooks, memory, profile builder, plus an embedded `hermes --tui` chat. Secured behind an OAuth/token gate.\n- **Ink TUI** (`hermes --tui` or `display.interface: tui`) — terminal UI with docked widget apps — `references/tui-widgets.md`.\n- **OpenAI-compatible proxy** (`hermes proxy`) — a local OpenAI API backed by whichever OAuth provider you're signed into. Point Codex CLI, Aider, Cline, or any script at it — no API key.\n\n## Hard Invariants (never violate, regardless of what you loaded)\n\n- **Never break prompt caching** — don't change past context, toolsets, or the system prompt mid-conversation. The only exception is context compression.\n- **Message role alternation** — never two assistant or two user messages in a row; only `tool` results can repeat.\n- **Secrets in `.env`, settings in `config.yaml`** — never tell a user to put a non-credential setting in `.env`.\n- **Profile-safe paths** — `get_hermes_home()` in code, `$HERMES_HOME` when resolving paths in a session.\n- **Never hand-edit `config.yaml` for the user** — use `hermes config set KEY VAL`; a stray indent can corrupt the file and break the live gateway.\n", "path": "autonomous-ai-agents/hermes-agent/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/sergio/.hermes/skills/autonomous-ai-agents/hermes-agent", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/background-systems.md", "references/cli-reference.md", "references/providers-and-models.md", "references/portal-auth-for-third-party-apps.md", "references/tui-widgets.md", "references/project-context-files.md", "references/petdex.md", "references/slash-commands.md", "references/delegate-ta[API-KEY REDATTA].md", "references/windows-quirks.md", "references/webhooks.md", "references/contributor-guide.md", "references/configuration.md", "references/native-mcp.md", "references/desktop-plugins.md", "references/security-privacy.md", "references/themes.md", "references/troubleshooting.md"], "templates": ["templates/skin.yaml"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["hermes", "setup", "configuration", "multi-agent", "spawning", "cli", "gateway", "bots", "bot-mode", "features", "themes", "skins", "desktop-plugins", "tui-widgets", "petdex", "development"], "homepage": "https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent", "related_skills": ["claude-code", "codex", "opencode"]}}}

### Due cose per te

1. **Vuoi allineare omarchy a mc2** (stesso codice su entrambe, canale `main`)? Comando: `hermes update --set-channel main` + update — riavvia il suo gateway (Merlino). Dimmi "vai" e lo faccio.
2. Note minori: il **backup pre-update è disattivato** in config (`updates.pre_update_backup: off` — c'è comunque lo snapshot rapido; dimmi se lo riattivo), e ci sono **4 autostash vecchi (>7 giorni)** con modifiche locali mai ripristinate sul checkout di mc2 — se vuoi te li faccio vedere per decidere restore o drop.
